RAJYA SABHA 
Wednesday, the 8th February, 2023/19 Magha, 1944 (Saka) 
The House met at one minute past eleven of the clock, 
MR. CHAIRMAN in the Chair. 
ANNOUNCEMENT BY THE CHAIR 
MR. CHAIRMAN: Hon. Members, as agreed to by the House yesterday, there will be no 
Zero Hour, no Question Hour and further Discussion on the Motion of Thanks on 
President's Address will start immediately after laying of the Papers and other Business 
of formal nature and will continue withou t lunch break. The Answers to the Questions 
listed for today are, however, deemed to have been laid on the Table of the House.

SHRI N.R. ELANGO (Tamil Nadu): Hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, I thank you for the 
opportunity given to me to speak on the hon. President's Address.  Sir, as per Article 87 
of the Constitution of India, the Special Addr ess of the hon. President is to inform the 
Parliament of the causes of the Summons. It is  always expected to inform the Parliament 
of the causes of the Summons, namely, what the Government was doing during the last 
year and what it is going to do next year . But I could see from the President's Address 
what they have been doing for all the nine ye ars and what they are visualizing for the next 
25 years. Sir, the great Thiruvalluvar said 
 "The mark of wisdom is to see the reality 
behind everything."  With that vision, when  I saw the President's Address, I wanted to 
make a mention about three or four points.  Democracy and federalism has been dealt 
with in paragraph 79. It says, "India's democracy was prosperous, strong, and will 
continue to be stronger in the future." The true federalism in real spirit and substance 
and not in ornamental form would be the seed for a vibrant democracy.  Democracy has 
to be practised, not to be preached. I can quote some examples where our democracy 
has been violated. The Union shall not at tempt centralization and should envisage 
decentralization by permitting the States to ex ercise powers of legislative and executive.  
The representatives of the Union Government in  the States should act as a catalyst and

SHRI NIRANJAN BISHI (Odisha): Hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, I stan d here to support 
the Motion of Thanks on the speech delivered by the hon. President of India, the first 
tribal woman President, who is the pride of tribals of India. However, on behalf of my 
party, the BJD, for the development of trib als of Odisha, I want to highlight some 
important issues concerned with my State of Odisha and demand attention of the Central 
Government on these important issues of Odisha.   
First is the inclusion of 169 tribal groups  or parts of such tribes pertaining to 
Odisha, as recommended by our hon. Chief Mi nister, Shri Naveen Patnaik. The left-out 
Scheduled Tribes are Saara, Tamadia, Bhanja Puran, Kalanga, Paharia, Jhodia, 
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
71

Mankidia, Durua, Chuktia Bhunjia, Paudi Bhuyan, Amanatia, Beldar Gond, Budu Kandh, 
Boda Savara, Bhogta, Boi Gadaba, Bareng Jhodia Paraja, Cherenga Kolha, Chapua 
Kamar, etc. Such types of hundreds of tribal communities, which are proposed for 
inclusion, are actually subsets or phonetic va riations of the name of the existing ST 
communities, but are still deprived of the benefits availed by STs with similar tribal 
characteristics residing in different parts of the State, who have since been notified as 
STs. Inclusion of the left-out communities wi ll give them their much-needed recognition 
as Scheduled Tribes and ensure them social justice in accordance with the provisions of 
the Constitution of India.  
Sir, second is, withdrawal of 18 per cent GST on Kendu leaves. Our hon. Chief 
Minister of Odisha, Shri Naveen Patnaik, ha s been demanding for withdrawal of 18 per 
cent GST on Kendu leaf in the greater interest of the State of Odisha as more than eight 
lakh poor tribal people associated with the trade are getting affected. The tribal people 
collect the leaves as part of their right defined under the Scheduled Tribes and Other 
Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act, 2006. Imposition of 18 per 
cent GST on Kendu leaves is adversely affect ing the trade. This, in turn, affects the 
livelihoods of Kendu leave pluckers, bi nders and seasonal workers, and the 
implementation of the social security and welfare schemes for them. In the interest of the 
livelihood of the community, it is urged upon the Government to withdraw the imposition 
of GST on Kendu leaves for the greater interest of the State of Odisha. 
Third is, inclusion of tribal languages in th e Eighth Schedule of the Constitution of 
India. Odisha has the third largest tribal po pulation in the country. As per Census 2011, 
40 per cent of the population in Odisha belongs to Scheduled Tribe and Scheduled Caste 
communities. The State is home to 13 Partic ularly Vulnerable Tribal Groups (PVTGs). 
Language remains a huge obstacle for the attainment of education for tribal children. 
Learning should necessarily also consider th e pertinence of each medium of instruction 
for different linguistic and cu ltural backgrounds. The Government should focus on 
developing multilingual pedagogic instruments,  teaching-learning materials in the local 
dialect considering the tribal socio-cultural surrounding. Odisha's languages continue to 
be disregarded. We demand that the tribal languages like Ho, Bhumij and Mundari be 
included in the Eighth Schedule of the Constitution of India. Fourthly, the Tribal Sub Plan 
and KBK project should be restored and Budget provision should be made for 
development of tribals of Odisha.

SHRI BIKASH RANJAN BHATTACHARYYA (West Bengal): Thank you, Chairman, Sir, 
for giving me this opportunity to deliberate on the address of the Hon. President of India.  
I offer my thanks to the Hon. President of India but I really cannot offer any thanks or give 
any credence to the speech which she has ma de. If you kindly go through the entire 
speech and the lines in between, we find this  is not a speech which reflects the Indian 
constitutional ethos or constitutional morality. It really reflects the idea of Hinduism; it 
reflects the basic idea of BJP which wants to really establish a Hindu Rashtra following 
78
[RAJYA SABHA]

the RSS guideline, without implementing the In dian constitutional goal. This is based on 
misrepresentation of ideas and facts. You see,  it is said that the Rajpath is now the 
Kartavya Path because Rajpath is the symbol of colonialism. I don’ t know wherefrom do 
they get this fact.  During the colonial period, it was Kingsway and Queensway. That had 
been changed to Janpath and Rajpath. 'Raj' is  not the colonial word. 'Raj' is a concept 
which is available in the Indian cultural history. Now, you want to take a false credit and 
try to pose it as anti-colonial, though your hi story to fight the colonial Government is very 
poor. Now, again you find throughout the speech, there are references of many 
scholars. But, being the country of multicultural heritage; we have the heritage of 
different ideas. But this speech only contains the ideas of Hindu leaders, Hindu seers.  
What do you mean to communicate? Do you want to keep the country united on the 
constitutional principle? And, your intention is  to divide the country on the religious line 
which is the practice, unfortunately, now be ing followed by the Government. You see, 
you have a false claim. I don’t know who had advised the Hon. President to make this 
statement on triple talaq! Triple talaq was the contribution of the Hon. Supreme Court of 
India.  Hon. Supreme Court of India had declared this unconstitutional. The Government 
doesn’t have any credit to this. They were bound to make a law after the declaration of 
the law made by the hon. Supreme Court. But, here it is said, 'the Government has the 
credit from abrogation of Article 370 to triple talaq. Why do you want to communicate this 
false information to the country through the President of India? 
Now you say that we have poverty eradicat ion. Kindly see the Constitution which 
wants the scientific temper. Here, you are really trying to communicate the most 
unauthentic, unscientific idea. Poverty does bring illness. It says, ‘poverty is illness!’ For 
instance, it says, ‘the major cause of poverty is illness.’ Is it right? Is it not illness 
because of poverty? You want to put the cart before the horse! You don’t want to 
eradicate poverty but you want to eradicate illness. This is a wrong concept by which you 
can’t neither eradicate poverty nor take care of illness. In the name of taking care of 
illness, you want to take credit for the Ayushmaan Bharat. What has the Ayushmaan 
Bharat done? You have claimed that 80 crore people have to be fed with five kilogram of 
rice. Does it reflect the confidence in the country’s economy? Or, does it reflect your 
weakness? You want to take credit out of no thing. The President of India, unfortunately, 
was made to read the statement.  
Kindly see how you have destroyed the country in its eco-balance. You claim that 
our heritage connects to our roots with projects like Ayodhya Dham and so many 
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
79

Dhams. But you don’t mention that Joshimath is  sinking because of your unwise decision 
to construct roads for the purpose of pilgrimage and make them picnic spots. This sinking 
of Joshimath is another reflection of your de stroying the eco-balance of the country. This 
eco-balance of the country has to be mainta ined while you are making constructions, 
ecobalance can not be destroyed for the purpose of Hindu pilgrimage.  
Again you say that it is a slave mentality. If the Rajpath is a slave mentality, then 
what would be your discharge of duty to the people of the country? You have to discharge 
the Raj Dharma which you don’t do. Kartavya Path means that which you have decided to 
do. That is reflected through the speeches lik e Hindu nationalism. Against that, we have 
been fighting and we are inviting all the people to get together.  
Sir, the strength of the Government lies in its power to face the Opposition. If you 
are an honest Government, you should not get scared of any investigation. Now the entire 
House is demanding a JPC to have an investigation in the affairs of a particular corporate 
house. Why do you get scared of it? Sir, will the Chair direct the Treasury Benches that if 
it is honest, it should go in for a JPC? Let there be an investigation and let the 
Government face it. Without this, the President’s Address would take us to nowhere.  
SHRI ANEEL PRASAD HEGDE (Bihar): Mr. Chairm an, Sir, I rise to speak on the Motion 
of Thanks on the President's Address. First of all, on certain issues, I want to say that the

SHRI AJIT KUMAR BHUYAN (Assam):  Thank you, Mr. Chairman, Sir.  Since I belong to 
Assam and being the lone voice of Opposition  from the North-Eastern Region in this 
Upper House, I will not speak on major concerns of inflation and unemployment gripping 
across the nation, as my learned colleague s have already enlightened us on those 
issues, and the time constraint is also there. So, I would like to continue my intervention 
on the specific concerns of the North-Eastern Region.  As this was the first time when I 
listened to the Address, by Mahamahim Droupadi Murmu ji given in the joint sitting of 
both the Houses, as a Member of Parliament, I had a very high expectation that she 
would dwell at ethnic diversity of the North-Eastern Region and its complexity.   
 Assam was a single unit of governance, except Tripura and Manipur, when India 
got independence in 1947, and it was very rightly debated in the Constituent Assembly 
that ethnic diversity of the region would be protected and even the small tribes and their 
identity and culture would be protected in  the post-independence era. The tribal 
community of the North-East region had hoped that their identity and culture would be 
protected under the guidance of Mahamahim Rashtrapati ji who herself is a tribal. Sir, I 
am very sorry to say that now tribals in A ssam are facing illegal eviction. The insurgency 
and conflict in the region have been the result  of not addressing the core issue of ethnic 
diversity. The people of the region had developed some hope when the Prime Minister 
told the nation that the Naga conflict was resolved when the Government of India inked 
the framework agreement in August, 2015 but the President's Address does not reflect 
any concern on the Naga conflict.   
 Of course, some development projects have been initiated but it is not a mercy 
but it is the legitimate right of the North Eastern Region. However, peace and 
development of the region will be sustainabl e when the Government of India addresses 
the very core issue. Sir, please allow me to highlight the very promise made by the Prime 
Minister, Shri Narendra Modi. He assured that six communities of Assam will be given 
88
[RAJYA SABHA]

special Scheduled Tribe status but, Sir, even when the second tenure of the Government 
is almost coming to an end, no such in tent has been reflected in the President's 
Address.    
 Sir, a high-level committee headed by the retired Justice of Guwahati High Court 
was formed by the Ministry of Home Affairs in July, 2019 to examine the effectiveness of 
actions taken since 1985 to implement clause  6 of the Assam Acco rd, and to suggest 
and recommend measures, as may be nece ssary, to protect, preserve and promote 
cultural, social, linguistic identity and he ritage of the Assamese people. Sir, the 
Committee had submitted its report in Fe bruary, 2020 but this report has not been 
released so far and nothing has been ment ioned in the President's Address for the 
indigenous people of Assam.  
 Sir, during the General Elections in 201 4, in a public meeting in Assam, Shri 
Narendra Modi promised that within 100 days, every problem of Assam will be solved but 
nothing has been solved so far. Sir, I fail to understand that the two powerful Cabinet

concerns of the tribal.  Have they become voi celess or scared of the mighty notion of the 
duo, Narendra Modi and Amit Shah. Sir, in both the Houses, the region is represented

Singh, for his novel initiative of development of the region though he was not frequently 
visiting the region. Sir, now, projects are bein g inaugurated.  But, as the Leader of the 
Opposition rightly said, those projects were initiated by Dr. Manmohan Singh while they 
are now simply inaugurating these projects.  Sir, I will conclude with a famous saying that 
the human being is not known by physical  appearance but by his honestly-conceived 
intent, ideas and actions.  Thank you.

Motion of Thanks on the President's Address.  I support it with all my heart.   
 Hon. Vice-Chairman Madam, the hon. President, in her speech, has said, "Our 
heritage connects us to our roots and our development gives us the courage to reach out 
to the sky. That is why my Government has chosen the path of consolidating heritage 
and giving priority to development." So, heritage and modernity, and heritage and 
development go hand in hand. And it has been  proven time and again that civilisational 
cultures, which do not have deep roots, fall and crumble. Why has our civilisation 
survived for millennia? Perhaps the reason is  that our roots are very, very deep and I 
would like to reiterate them. On the one hand, we are following the path shown by saints 
like Adi Shankaracharya, Shri Basaveshwara Swamy, Thiruvalluvar, Guru Nanak Dev and 
so many others; on the other hand, today, India is also becoming a hub of hi-tech 
technology. So, again, I would like to reiterate and give some examples of how 
preserving India's glorious past is leading us to the present and then to the future. This is 
where the vision of our hon. Prime Minister, Sh ri Narendra Modi, is guiding the nation in 
every direction. For example, under Narendra Modiji's leadership, it has been decided 
that five iconic museums of the country will be made of international standards as the 
Central Government is working to put the cult ural potential of India in a new image in 
front of the world so that India can become a big centre of heritage tourism, which is 
very, very important.  This is not only to preserve but to showcase and to arouse interest 
in the world, in the nation and in the people to feel pride. The nation builders in these 75 
years, the first being the Indian Museum in Ko lkata, which is, perhaps, one of the oldest 
museums in the world and the five iconic museums would be in different parts of India.   
Coming to pay tributes to India's great freedom fighters, on the one hand, we 
have the heritage and on the other hand, we come down to our own times, our own

[RAJYA SABHA]

centuries, the past 3-4 centuries specially, as we celebrate 'Azadi ka Amrit Mahotsav' or 
75 years of freedom remembering the freedom fighters and paying tributes to them. This

freedom fighters, their soldiers and every sing le individual, who helps in not only building 
the country, the nation, but also protecting, if  we forget them, then, that is the biggest 
crime.  Here, we are paying tribute to India's great freedom fighters in the 'Azadi ka Amrit 
Mahotsav'.  It is very significant how we do it, how Mahatma Gandhi's dream is brought 
to life.  While the 150 th Birth Anniversary of Mahatma Gandhi was celebrated globally led

due to 'Swachh Bharat Mission'. I think many of my fellow hon. Members have also 
spoken about it. The first time when he spoke from the Red Fort on 15 th August on

a Prime Minister speak about all these things from the ramparts of Red Fort, but that had 
an all India implication and today it has carried the message to every corner of India.   
Then, there is the Statue of Unity. The fo rgotten name of Sardar Patel, who was a

Nagpur, where Nizam signed the document of accession. So, it is Sardar Patel who is 
being paid tribute by erecting the tallest statue in the world, which is 182 metres tall.

THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (SHRI V. VIJAYASAI REDDY): Shri Birendra Prasad Baishya. 
 
SHRI BIRENDRA PRASAD BAISHYA (Assam): Sir, I, on behalf of my party Asom Gana 
Parishad, rise here today to support the Motion of Thanks on the President’s Address. 
 Sir, I am proud that I am an Assamese. I am proud that I belong to the North-
Eastern Region. I do feel proud that I am an  Indian. During the Indian freedom struggle, 
like other parts of India, Assam and the North-Eastern Rsegion contributed much. I still 
remember, a 14 year school girl from Go hpur, Assam, who was trying to hoist the 
National Flag at Gohpur Police Station was killed by the British Army. I salute Kanaklata.  
Sir, in Amrit Kaal, I feel proud that the Join t Session of Indian Parliament was addressed 
by Her Excellency, hon. President of India, who belongs to a tribal community.  This is 
the respect our Government gives to the tribal people. This is the respect our 
Government gives to the backward people. 
 Sir, I would like to go back. When the British were ruling India, Asia’s first oil 
refinery was established at Digboi in Assam. It  was the first refinery in Asia. From 1947 to 
2023, days are going very fast and the country is also developing very fast. After 
independence, Pandit Jawahar Lal Nehru became the Prime Minister of India. After 
independence, rightly, genuinely, people of Assam demanded establishment of a refinery 
in Assam, but it was declined. A refinery was established at Barauni with the crude oil 
from Assam, when Pandit Jawahar Lal Nehru was the Prime Minister of the country. But 
it took a long time. After a long agitation of 13  years, in 1960, we got a refinery. In British 
raj, we got a refinery in Dibrugarh and in the independent Bharat, we got a refinery in 
Assam but it is a small refinery. This is just  one million tonne refinery. But it is known to 
everybody, Assam is a crude oil producing State.    
 Coming to poverty, I remember in 70’s, in our school life, there was a food crisis in 
our country. People were not getting food to eat.  There was no rice. There was no food.   
Now, we are self-sufficient. Not only are we self-sufficient, under the leadership of Prime 
Minister, Narendra Modiji, 80 crore people also got free ration during the Covid time.   
Under the leadership of Narendra Modiji, ‘sel f-sufficiency’ is not just a slogan, it has 
been implemented.  We are self-sufficient.  We remember during the Covid time, people 
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
129

were not getting masks, people were not getting sanitizers. Today, we are self-
sufficient. Now, India produces not only masks, sanitizers, but also its own vaccines.  
India is the only country in the world which vaccinated more than 200 crore people.   
Every time, we are speaking about China. Ch ina has emerged as a big power, but it 
failed to vaccinate the Chinese people, whereas Narendra Modi Government successfully 
vaccinated the people of our country.   
 In mobile industry, India is self-sufficient.  Not only self-sufficient, now India is also 
exporting mobiles. Now, I am coming to the North-Eastern Region.  In 2014, when Modiji 
took over as Prime Minister, he announced Nort h-Eastern Region as our priority sector.   
He not just announced, but also implemented it. ( Time bell rings ) Sir, give me two 
minutes. Before 2014, there was only one cancer hospital in Assam, Bhubaneswar 
Borooah Cancer Hospital. After Modiji took over, today, there are nine cancer hospitals 
in Assam, with the support and blessings of the Prime Minister and another 10 cancer 
hospitals are in the pipeline in Assam, under the leadership of Himanta Biswa Sarma, the 
dynamic Chief Minister of Assam. Sir, I know , my time is short, but I have to speak 
much. Since I know the Parliamentary decoru m, I cannot speak them all. I know your 
position, Sir. But one thing I would like to say here today is that achievement is 
achievement.  By criticising anybody, you cannot stop the achievement.    
 
THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (SHRI V.VIJAYASAI REDDY): Please conclude, Birendraji. 
 
SHRI BIRENDRA PRASAD BAISHYA: Sir, I would like to make one more point and, with 
that, I would conclude. Now, India is movi ng towards becoming one of the developed 
countries in the world. I hope under the leadership of hon. Prime Minister, in the coming 
days, India would be growing and developing more. With these words, thank you, Mr. 
Vice-Chairman, Sir, for allowing me to speak.

and action for the development of the country  connecting every nook and corner and all 
sections of society under the indomitable leadership of Narendra Modiji. 
 The thought process of the Government of India "Sabka Saath, Sabka Vikash, 
Sabka Vishwas and Sabka Prayas"  has become the source of inspiration and Kartavya 
Path of development of great India. 
 Sir, there was a time when the people  of North-Eastern Region always felt 
deprived and ignored by the Government of India in every sphere of life. But, now-a-
days, under the leadership of Shri Narendra Modi ji, the feeling of deprivation and 
ignorance has switchover to feeling of care  and experiencing a new dawn of peace and 
development. 
 Sir, as a result of successful steps taken by the Government of India in containing 
the difficult situation of unrest and terrorism,  the North-Eastern Region is now moving in 
the path of peace and development. Sir, signing of peace accord with Bodo, Karbi and 
other ethnic groups and speedy implementati on of accord itself shows the positive 
attitude and care of the Government of In dia towards the people of North-Eastern 
Region. 
 The policies and actions taken by the Government of India for the development of 
North-Eastern Region, which are reflected in the Address of the President, are 
appreciable and adorable. I express our appreciation and gratitude to the Government of 
India.   
 Looking forward to a prosperous, inclusive and developed India under the 
leadership of Shri Narendra Modi ji, I conclude my speech supporting the President's 
Address.  Thank you, Sir.

reach out to the region. Today, the North Eastern Region is getting its due share of 
attention and affection that it deserves.  As a witness to the unprecedented development 
and progress across the region, most importan tly, it must be stated that the North 
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
163

Eastern Region is now emotionally integrated with the rest of the country.  The North 
Eastern Region is poised to play a bigger role in the years to come.  With the Act East 
Policy, which earlier was the Look East Policy, the North Eastern Region will play an 
increasingly important role in the developmen t of the country as a whole.  India is now 
going through a very crucial phase where the next 25 years will set the tone for India's 
growth and progress.  In this journey, th e North Eastern Region will shift from the 
periphery to the mainstream and contribute towards national progress.   
 In health sector, one of the most revolu tionary schemes introduced by the Central 
Government is the Ayushmaan Bharat Yojana.  In my State, Nagaland, the beneficiaries 
of this scheme are over 2.3 lakh households.  I have personally seen the impact that this 
scheme has made in the lives of the people who now have access to quality healthcare.  
You go to any place in Nagaland and you will hear people saying, "Modi ki bishi khusi 
paishey", which means, "We are very grateful to Modi."  It is a fact that hon. Prime 
Minister Modi ji is hugely popular across th e North Eastern States because of his wise 
and astute leadership.  
 
(MR. CHAIRMAN in the Chair.) 
 
 When the hon. President spoke about th e infrastructural development, the North 
Eastern Region has been a major beneficiary of infrastructural development. I was also

Meghalaya during the Golden Jubilee celeb rations of the North Eastern Council. In 
Assam, the Bogibeel bridge connects Assa m and Arunachal and it is a standing 
testimony of the infrastructural development taking place in the North East. While in 
Manipur, the world's tallest Pier Bridge is nearing completion and also the first National 
Sports University is set to be established in Manipur. We see the commitment of Modi 
Government in the North East.  In Nagaland, we now have two medical colleges coming 
up, one in Kohima and one in Mon District.  Connectivity in Nagaland has also increased.   
 Sir, for a small State like Nagaland where agriculture is the mainstay of the people, 
its famous ghost chilli, the king chilli has been exported.  A total of 2,04,477 beneficiaries 
have registered under the PM Kisan Samman  Nidhi scheme in Nagaland. Similarly, 
another North-Eastern State, Tripura, is seeing a lot of positives in the field of agriculture 
as a result of pro-active policies pursued by the Modi Government. In fact, in 2022, 
164
[RAJYA SABHA]

Tripura exported pineapples worth Rs. 14 crores to Dubai, Qatar and Bangladesh 
besides exporting other fruits and horticulture products to countries like England and 
Germany. It was the first of its kind waterway consignment. Kisan Rail and Kisan UDAN 
schemes of the Central Government are benefitting the farmers of Tripura immensely.   
 In the pursuit of making India the logistics hub of the world, the introduction of 
PM-DevINE scheme, which is the Prime Mini ster's Development Initiative for North 
Eastern Region will play a massive role. It aims at rapid and holistic development of the 
North Eastern Region by funding infrastructure and social development projects. In the 
spirit of PM Gati Shakti scheme, it will ensu re livelihood for the youth of the region and fill 
the developmental gaps in various sectors.   
 On the Naga political issue, which has been mentioned twice today in the debate 
on the Motion of Thanks, the people of Nagala nd are grateful to the hon. Prime Minister

been going on for decades have proactivel y moved forward under hon. Prime Minister 
Modi ji and we are hopeful that the negotiati ng parties will come to a conclusion soon 
with a solution that is honourable, acceptable and sustainable.   
 Women empowerment has been the hallmark of the Modi Government. As the 
first woman MP to represent my State here in  this august House after Nagaland attained 
Statehood in 1963, I am an example of inclus ive governance. Successive earlier political 
parties and Governments never thought of this. But under the double engine 
Government of Narendra Modi ji, today, I am here representing my State. Also, I would 
like to thank the hon. Prime Minister for his inclusive vision that has brought in here a 
luminary, an icon in the field of sports an d a small time worker like me, from the most 
backward area of Nagaland. As mentioned earlier, after 100 years, we are getting a 
second railway station which had been commissioned in Shokhuvi last year.        
 With gratitude, I accept the congratulati ons of an hon. senior Member because 
my election here has given a lot of hope to the women of Nagaland. It has also given a lot 
of hope to the women across the North-Eastern Region and also the well-wishers across 
the country. I accept the congratulations without the rider that followed.   
 Harnessing nari shakti has been a crucial factor in  moving India upward in the 
development index. In the North-Eastern States, schemes like Ujjawala Yojana and Jal 
jeevan Mission have brought much relief to our mothers, sisters and daughters.   
 Women empowerment is about many th ings, including cr eating an enabling 
environment for women to come forward. It is about opening sainik schools for female 
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
165

students; it is about commiss ioning women officers in the Armed Forces; it is about 
bringing in women in the Cabinet with key portfolios like External Affairs, Defence and 
Finance; it is about celebrating the girl child  with schemes like Beti Bachao Beti Padhao 
which has led to the increase in female sex ratio of 1020 for every 1000 males. 
 As I conclude, I would like to quot e some figures because this morning 
educational institutions were also talked about. Female students outnumber their male 
counterparts at every level of higher educat ion in Nagaland. The All-India Survey on 
Higher Education for 2021, released on 29
th January by the Union Ministry of Education, 
reflected that out of 46,954 students enrolled  at various levels in Nagaland, there were 
21,750 males and 25,204 females. Overall, enrolment in higher education institutions rose 
from 37,125 in 2012-13 to 46,954 in 2020-21 which represent an increase of 26.51 per 
cent over the past decade. Nationwide male students slightly outnumber female 
students. However, in the North-Eastern Region, female enrolment stands at 6.14 lakhs 
in 2021, which is higher than the male enrolment of 5.92 lakhs. This strength has been 
seen since 2018-19. In 2012-13, there were four universities in Nagaland. There was one 
Central and one Open University as well as  two private universities and 59 colleges. In 
2020-21, the number of universities increased to five with an addition of one private 
university and the number of colleges has gone up to 68. All these facts and figures show 
that the North-Eastern Region is now on the path of peace, progress and development 
under hon. Prime Minister, Narendra Modi ji. With these words, I welcome and support 
the President's Address. Thank you, Sir.

Need for assistance to RINL for better functioning of Vishakhapatnam Steel Plant 
 
SHRI G.V.L. NARASIMHA RAO (Uttar Pradesh): Sir, due to non-availability of working 
material, high raw-material cost and advers e market conditions, the capacity utilization 
of Rashtriya Ispat Nigam Limited has been lower this year which adversely impacted the 
finances of the company. Banks have not b een extending loans to RINL due to its low 
credit rating.  Further, rate of interest char ged to RINL by banks is significantly higher 
than market lending rates. At present, only two of the three blast furnaces are 
operational and funds are required for arrang ing the oxygen supply to operationalise the 
third furnace and to increase its production substantially.   
The employees' morale is also low at this time as the wage revision for workers in 
RINL has not been done since 2017, although an MoU was signed between management 
and employee unions on 6
th April, 2022. The promotion of executives has been held up, 
though its financial impact on the company is very limited. To help RINL improve its 
financials and to ensure efficient functionin g of RINL, it has to have working capital 
available. This can be arranged internally by divesting some non-core assets like the 
forged wheel plant at Rae Bareli and 22 acres land plot in Visakhapatnam city. This will 
provide immediate liquidity for the RINL to in crease RINL’s production. But, a stronger 
impetus or infusion of funds is required to ensure that the RINL becomes fully operational 
and profitable. RINL is the largest industrial unit in Andhra Pradesh and its performance 
is vital for State’s economy. Thank you. 
 
DR. SASMIT PATRA (Odisha): Sir, I associate myself with the submission made by the 
hon. Member.

Need for reviving of Sethusamudram Shipping Canal Project 
 
SHRI P. WILSON (Tamil Nadu): Sir, Sethusamudram Shipping Canal Project is a 150 
years dream of Tamils, including Perarignar Anna. The project proposes to make a sea 
interface between Palk Bay and Gulf of Mannar so that ships do not need to go around 
Sri Lanka and will facilitate movement of sh ips like Panama and Suez Canals and boost 
economic growth. It will also help fishermen travel from Gulf of Mannar to Palk Strait, 
prevent transshipment of Indian goods at ports in Sri Lanka, besides cutting short sailing 
of an additional distance 254-424 nautical miles and save 21-36 hours of sailing time 
besides fuel cost, provide jobs to more than 10 lakh people. 
Particularly now when Sri Lankan Ports ar e coming under the influence of China, 
time is right to prevent transhipment of Indian goods through these ports. The project will 
also help both coastal and international mariti me trade in Tamil Nadu and coastal States.  
Another benefit is that the canal will enhanc e national defence and security by enabling 
easier access between the coasts for the Western and the Eastern fleets. 
 The Tamil Nadu State Assembly, on the mo tion moved by the hon. Chief Minister, 
Shri M.K. Stalin, unanimously adopted a resolution to implement the project without any 
further delay. 
 I, therefore, request the hon. Prime Minister to take all efforts to revive the 
Sethusamudram project to ensure India’s national security and boost economic growth.

Need for addressing human animal conflicts in Kerala 
 
SHRI SANDOSH KUMAR P (Kerala): The human-animal conflict has become a serious 
concern in Kerala. Almost every day, the deaths  of people due to tiger, elephant or wild 
pig attacks were reported from the three most affected districts in Kerala, namely, 
Wayanad, Idukki and Palakkad. The farmers in  these districts often witness the entry of 
animals into their farms which has made their life and livelihood under threat. The wildlife 
population in Kerala’s forests has exploded in recent years and there is no space left for 
them despite the “increase in forest cover.” According to the official data, Kerala had 190 
tigers, of which 80 are in the Wayanad Sanc tuary. According to the Kerala Forest 
Department, in 2021-22, 144 compensation cases were filed for human casualties due to 
animal attacks. Wild pigs, elephants, and tigers have killed people, and regularly 
trespass on farmers’ properties and rob them  of their produce. The farmers have been 
182
[RAJYA SABHA]

demanding that the Government should allow them to cull wild animals.  But the Wild Life 
Protection Act denies humans the right to de fend themselves from animal attacks.  
Under WLPA, people are not free to defend themselves against raiding animals and 
driving them out of their homes and the agricu ltural field needs official permission. While 
admirable in their intent, these strict legal prov isions make it very difficult for local people 
to deal with life threatening situations of human-animal conflict. Therefore, there is an 
urgent need to implement the most pragmati c policy that can effectively address the 
sentiments of the people in these districts.

Time consuming and prolonged organ donation procedures in the country 
 
SHRI TIRUCHI SIVA (Tamil Nadu): Sir, organ donation in India is regulated by the 1994 
Transplantation of Human Organs and Tissues Act. The Act aims to control the storage 
and transplantation of organs and prevents an y commercial dealings.  At the same time, 
the National Organ and Tissue Transplant Organization (NOTTO) facilitates the 
procurement, allotment and distribution of organs within India. 
 However, despite such legislations, there is a disparity between this colossal 
demand and low supply. About five lakh people  in India die every year from causes that 
could have been prevented by organ transplants. Organ donation rates in India stand at 
0.86 per million population, a miniscule figure in comparison to countries like Spain, 
which stands at 46.9 per million population. If India's donation rates were to be improved 
to, at least, one donation per million deaths, it would satisfy the country's organ 
requirement. 
 Despite the convenient donor registrati on process wherein any person willing to 
donate his/her organs can do so by filling out the donor consent form, organ donation is 
associated with numerous difficulties, namely , lack of awareness and the spiritual belief 
of life after death. 
 I urge the Government of India to come together with NGOs, CSOs, and non-
State stakeholders to not only spread awareness and dispel myths and apprehensions in 
the minds of the people regarding organ donation, but also to simplify the rules for 
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
185

cadaver organ donations and transplantations  to save precious lives through timely 
intervention.

Need to allow writing both in Bengali and Meitei scripts in UPSC for optional paper of 
Manipuri literature  
 
SHRI MAHARAJA SANAJAOBA LEISHEMBA (Manipur): Sir, in UPSC/IAS Exams, there 
are two optional papers I and II in Manipuri Literature with a total mark of 500 (250+250).  
At present, only the Bengali script is allowed to write the papers of Manipuri Literature.  
As a matter of State policy, the Government of Manipur has introduced Meitei script for 
writing Manipuri language since 2006 in place of Bengali script in phase-wise manner.  
Colleges and Universities in Manipur have started producing B.A. and M.A. written in 
Meitei script for Manipuri language. Now, the problem is that those students who passed 
B.A./M.A. written in Meitei script in Manipuri subjects are not getting the chance of 
appearing UPSC/IAS Main Exams for Manipuri optional papers as they did not learn 
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
187

Bengali script. Hence, they are being depriv ed of appearing from UPSC Main Exams' 
Manipuri optional papers. The students are demanding to allow them to appear 
UPSC/IAS Manipuri papers in Meitei script.  The issue has become a serious problem of 
the State as their demand is genuine and quite logical. To solve this burning issue, I 
request the Government of India, through this House, to allow writing both in Bengali and 
Meitei scripts in UPSC/IAS Manipuri Literature optional papers by amending the UPSC 
Main Exam Manipuri Literature Papers' instruction as "Answer must be written in 
Manipuri (Bengali or Meitei scripts)" instead of the present instruction "Answer must be 
written in Manipuri (Bengali script)".

Issues faced by ASHA workers 
 
DR. V. SIVADASAN (Kerala): Sir, the accredited social health activists in the country are 
doing remarkable works in rural India. There are 10.4 lakh ASHA workers in our nation.  
The World Health Organisation has appreciated them as global health leaders. This was 
a proud moment for the whole nation. But th eir allowances and incentives are very 
minimal. Even these minimal allowances are not disbursed on time. They are assisting 
the poor and needy people in their most difficult times by giving health care. But the 
ASHA workers are not getting any assistance from the Union Government to care about 
their own health. Every day, they are spendi ng long hours for the health care of the 
persons in their locality. But even a minimal insurance package is not arranged for them.  
They are not given even minimum wages.

[RAJYA SABHA]

salary as employees because they are the social security volunteers. The Government is 
not treating it as a job. The Union Government should provide social security to the social 
security workers. They should be given job security and health facilities.

Need for Menstrual and Maternal leaves for women in higher education  
 
SHRI A.A. RAHIM (Kerala): Sir, the Higher Education Department of Kerala Government 
released an order granting menstrual and matern ity leave in higher education institutions.  
Menstrual leave will be through a 2 per cent relaxation in attendance and maternity leave of 
60 days is ordered. This landmark decision will have a great positive impact for young 
women students.   
[ 8 February, 2023 ]
189

 It is a step in the direction towards en abling a more equal space for education for 
women.  It is also an important step which will enable conversation about menstruation.  
It is a completely natural biological process which is stigmatized in our societies.  Women 
have been treated as impure and polluting. This kind of thinking is unbecoming of a 
modern democratic society. This is also a reason for the lower rates of access to 
education for women.   
 A policy of menstrual leave will have far-reaching impact in transforming attitudes 
towards educating girl children as well. Th is policy must be implemented across the 
country in higher education institutions. The Central Ministry should take this up and 
bring about a policy and implement it across the Central institutions and encourage other 
States where such a policy does not exist.   
 Further, it is high time that the issue of  menstrual leave in all the sectors is also 
brought up for discussion. For this, the Government must carry out studies to 
understand the difficulties f aced by working women in various sectors with regard to 
menstruation and what methods would be best  to lessen their difficulties. I hope the 
Government takes this issue seriously and takes the necessary steps.

Need for upgradation of AIR Kokrajhar at par with AIR Guwahati and establishment of a 
branch of news unit for broadcast of news in the Bodo language under Regional News 
Unit (RNU), AIR Guwahati 
 
SHRI RWNGWRA NARZARY (Assam): Sir, the All India Radio, Kokrajhar, commissioned 
on 15th August, 1999, with Bodo as the link lang uage is the only programme-originating 
station of lower Assam situated at Kokrajhar, capital of the Bodoland Territorial Region.  
Since then, this station has been continuing  to produce and broadcast programmes in 
various formats in Bodo, Assamese and other dialects. The main purpose for 
establishment of this station was to bring up liftment of the vibrant culture of the Bodos 
and other communities and impart educative and informative knowledge to the people 
inhabited in its jurisdiction. Bodo is one of  the 22 languages included in the Eighth 
Schedule of the Indian Constitution and As sociate Official Language of Assam and a 
widely-spoken language, particularly in the Bodoland Territorial Region (BTR).   
 So, for giving due respect and importance to the Bodo language and progress of 
cultures and literatures of all sections of the communities within its jurisdiction, it is quite 
desirable to upgrade the AIR, Kokrajhar, at  par with capital stations. As the Bodo 
language is widely spoken language within the jurisdiction of AIR, Kokrajhar, it will be 
very beneficial for the listeners, if a branch of the news unit is established under the 
Regional News Unit (RNU), AIR, Guwahati, for broadcast of news items in Bodo. This 
will help the Bodo-knowing people in better understanding of circulated information and 
news items.   
 So, I urge upon the Minister of Information and Broadcasting to take measures for 
upgradation of AIR, Kokrajhar, at par with AIR, Guwahati, and establishment of a branch 
of News Unit at AIR, Kokrajhar.

Development has clubbed all the schemes wh ich include Integrated Child Development 
Services Scheme (ICDS), Pradhan Mantri Matru Vandana Yojana (PMMVY) and the 
National Creche Scheme (NCS). But the budgetary allocation for Saksham Anganwadi 
and Poshan Schemes is only 0.5 per cent of the total Budget.   
 India ranks 101
 out of 116 countries in the Global Hunger Index, 2021. According to 
National Family Health Survey-5 (NFHS-5) da ta, the rural population of stunted children 
in India is 37 per cent and in urban areas, it  is 30 per cent. The NFHS-5 survey also 
indicates that more than 57 per cent of women and over 67 per cent children suffer from 
anaemia. Therefore, there is a need to tack le these issues for mothers and children for 
holistic development of children during and after the pregnancy, and, hence reduce 
stunting with special emphasis on the marginalized sections.
